# PitFader — futures mean reversion results

PitFader (user 2026-11-01; called FuturesMr until it was named, PIT-1 / PIT-2 were FM-1 / FM-2): stock-style intraday
mean reversion on every futures root, the Fader family's futures member.

The engine: `TradingEdge.PitFader` (F#). The report: `scripts/futures/pitfader_report.py`. The bars: `data/futures/intraday_1s_ticks`
(rebuilt 2026-11-01 with the causal off-book list, 574 fills).

## PIT-1 — the bare-bones baseline (user 2026-11-01)

"Buy at 20m low, sell at 5m high and vice versa for shorts." FlushFader's channel rules on the BAR CLOCK (user: "We'll
stick to bar clock features for now"): long when the 1s vwap is strictly under the min of the prior 1,200 present bars
(once 1,200 bars are folded), out when it is strictly over the max of the prior 300; fills at the next present bar's
vwap; the mirror for shorts; one trip at a time per root and side. RTH only (user: liquidity, "for starters"): the bars
09:30–16:00 ET, entries before 15:00, a trip still open at the last bar exits there (eod). The contract = the previous
Globex / CFE session's volume leader (causal; no roll inside a day). Control: 15 of 15 sampled sessions identical to a
brute-force Python version. ⭐ WARMTH and the cutoff as in FlushFader / SpikeFader (2026-11-01): their channel counts the
signal bar itself (`entryMin.Count = WindowSize` after the push), so the first signal is the 1,200th bar, tested against
the 1,199 before it, and the 15:00 cutoff is inclusive (the first version waited one bar more: 292,804 trips, the same
PFs to two decimals).

```
  cd research && dotnet run --project TradingEdge.PitFader -c Release -- --max-concurrent 1 > logs/pitfader_mc1.log 2>&1; echo exit $?
  cd research && python -u scripts/futures/pitfader_report.py --trips data/futures/pitfader/trips_mc1 > data/futures/pitfader/report_mc1.md
```

**Verdict: a real but THIN reversion — PF ~1.05 gross, below 1 after one tick of cost.** 292,905 trips, 2015-01..2026-08.
- Gross in ticks the median root is PF 1.07; pooled in bp (equal notional) 1.05 both sides. 61–65 % winners, mean +0.3
  to +1.6 ticks a trip. One tick a round trip (a taker's ½ tick a side against the mid, before commissions) takes every
  root to ≤ 1.03 (NQ 1.03, MGC 1.03, 6S 1.03; the rest 0.57–0.99).
- The best gross: ZN / ZB 1.21 (but 1 tick = 1.3–2.1 bp there: 0.68 / 0.80 net), 6S 1.17, 6B 1.14, MES 1.14, ES 1.11, MGC
  1.11. The worst: MHG 0.70, SR3 0.87, RB 0.95, HE 0.96, ZT 0.95, VX 0.99 (thin roots, few trips).
- Long ≈ short. No year below 0.99 pooled; it IMPROVES lately (2025 1.12, 2026 1.11; 2015–2022 1.02–1.05).
- Time of day: 14:00–15:00 the best (1.10 pooled), 13:00 the worst (1.02).
- The equity-index group (ES / MES / NQ) is the most stable cell: 1.05–1.22 in every RTH hour block.
⇒ The bare system is the "PF ~1" case: it has to be filtered (the FlushFader / SpikeFader features, the volume
deltas — volatility first, as on stocks) or executed as a maker, not as a taker.

## PIT-2 — the same system as the mc = 0 SAMPLER (user 2026-11-01)

"Let's use mc = 0 for the research runs because they give us more data samples. We'll be able to better observe the
effects of filters on them." Every bar that makes a new 1,200-bar extreme is another trip (averaging down); every open
trip of the side exits at the first exit signal at or after its fill bar (shared exits, FlushFader's sampler).
`--max-concurrent 0` is now the engine's default; `--max-concurrent 1` is the PIT-1 book. Each trip records
`open_at_sig` (the trips of the side already open at its signal: the averaging-down depth).
**Controls:** the rewritten engine at mc = 1 == the PIT-1 engine (trip for trip, checked on the first version's 292,804); a GREEDY REPLAY of the mc = 0
trips by signal time (take a trip when its signal is at or after the last taken exit) == the mc = 1 book, trip for trip.

```
  cd research && dotnet run --project TradingEdge.PitFader -c Release -- --max-concurrent 0 > logs/pitfader_mc0.log 2>&1; echo exit $?
  cd research && python -u scripts/futures/pitfader_report.py --trips data/futures/pitfader/trips_mc0 > data/futures/pitfader/report_mc0.md
```

**5,746,017 trips (20× the book). Pooled gross PF 1.11 short / 1.14 long, about 0.96–0.99 after 1 tick.**
⭐ **THE DEPTH LADDER IS MONOTONE:** the deeper into an averaging-down run, the better — first trip 1.05 · 1–5 open 1.07 ·
6–25 1.11 · 26–50 1.15 (net of 1 tick 1.02) · 51–100 1.29 (net 1.17) · > 100 1.62 (net 1.53, 69,371 trips, mean +11.5
ticks). It is the stock systems' K-ladder (FlushFader K ∈ [26, 50]): the reversion pays after a long run of fresh
extremes, not at the first one. ⚠ The deep trips are CORRELATED — one long flush gives a hundred trips that all exit at
the same bar — so the sampler's PF weights each flush by its length; the episode-level count comes with the features
(the leg counters). Deep runs are presumably the volatile tape, which is the next feature to test.

## PIT-3 — the volatility floor per instrument, and volatility × depth (user 2026-11-01)

"For each instrument we should probably find a volatility floor where the expectancy of a trade starts to improve. Every
system will use the same features, but we'll find that different instruments enjoy different gate configurations."
**The stage-1 features are SEALED first** (`scripts/futures/pitfader_seal.fsx`): 40 RTH sessions over 28 roots fed to
FlushFader's IntradaySystem (longs) and SpikeFader's (shorts) with every gate at its OFF value, entries from 09:30, mc = 0
→ the signal sets are IDENTICAL (1,330 long / 1,826 short) and all 65 features they record agree exactly (0 mismatches);
the 9 columns no research engine records (delta_N_bar, vol_120/180_bar) match a brute-force sum (29,218 values, 0).
Control: volat_20m's half-life broken to 39 → the harness flags exactly volat_20m and its three derivatives (9,329).

```
  cd research && dotnet fsi scripts/futures/pitfader_seal.fsx -- <bars.csv> > logs/pitfader_seal.log 2>&1; echo exit $?
  cd research && python -u scripts/futures/pitfader_volat.py > data/futures/pitfader/volat.md 2> logs/pitfader_volat.log
```

volat_20m = the EWMA (half-life 40 slots of 30 present bars) of |ln| slot-vwap returns at the signal, in bp per slot.
Per root the floors are the root's OWN percentiles (instruments differ 10×+ in bp); pooled, volatility in TICKS
(volat_20m × price / tick). Net = −1 tick a round trip.

**Verdict: volatility is the lever here too, as on stocks — and it is what makes the depth ladder work.**
- Pooled by within-root decile, net PF climbs D1 0.89 → D5 1.01 → D9 1.06 → **D10 1.23** (+4.8 net ticks a trip); gross
  1.03 → 1.29. Most of the edge sits in the top decile.
- In TICKS: net expectancy turns positive at ≈ 5 ticks of 30-bar volatility (5–8: 1.02, 8–15: 1.05, ≥ 15: 1.11 with
  1/12 losing years); below 2 ticks every year loses net.
- Per root, a rising floor helps the equity indexes (ES 0.99 → p90 1.31 → p98 1.78, 0/7 losing years at p90; MES 1.05 →
  1.40, 0/8; NQ 1.07 → 1.29), the energies (MCL 1.06 → 1.43, CL 0.94 → 1.18, NG 1.03 → 1.28), 6E / 6J, ZN / ZT, VX (0.71
  → p95 1.70, 0/5) and MGC. It does NOT help 6B (peaks at p70), 6C, HG, HE or RB (flat until p95), and YM / MHG lose at
  every floor (one contract / 2024–26 only). The bp floors differ by root (table 2): ES p90 = 3.2 bp, CL 7.8, NG 15.3, VX
  32.6, ZN 1.8 — one feature, a different gate per instrument, as the user expected.
- ⭐ **VOLATILITY × DEPTH multiply.** In the calm deciles (D1–5) depth does nothing (net 0.93–1.02 at every depth); in D10
  the ladder is steep: 0.99 (first trip) → 1.24 (26–50 open) → 1.71 (51–100) → 3.85 (> 100), trip-weighted.
  ONE TRIP PER EPISODE (the trip with exactly k trips already open — knowable at the signal; each episode contributes at
  most one; an episode-AVERAGE was tried and rejected: it down-weights the losing runs, which are the long ones):
  D10 k = 25 → 1.17 (+3.6 net ticks, 8,278 trips) · **k = 50 → 1.37 (+7.4, 3,079 trips, every year 2015–2026 ≥ 1.07)** ·
  k = 100 → 2.91 (+33.0, 455). In D1–5 the same k = 50 trip is 0.92.
- ⚠ The percentile floors are FULL-SAMPLE per root: in-sample thresholds, and volatility regimes drift (the high-vol
  trips cluster in 2020 and 2022). The next step is a CAUSAL normaliser — volat_20m against the root's own trailing
  sessions — before any gate is fixed. And mc = 0 trips are correlated; the k-exact rows are the honest count.

## PIT-4 — stage 2 of the feature port: the bar and leg blocks, both sides, SEALED (user 2026-11-01)

The user: port by hand, side-neutral, "We'll also need equivalent features for the short system", and "we should make a
clean break from the stock trading systems" (volume delta and order-book features come next) — so PitFader owns its
engine and columns; the stock engines are only the reference each formula is sealed against.
- **BarBlock** (side-agnostic, `Features.fs`, 80 columns): rolling OLS of ln(vwap) at 60..1200 bars (accel = the
  600 − 1200 slope); the rolling vwaps and previous-window vwaps (speed = vwap / vwap_60_prev − 1); bar-count
  volume / trade-count / dollar sums (v10r = vol_10_bar / vol_60_bar × 6); the price moments (z20 from dlv_1200,
  dlv2_1200, vol_1200_bar); the session block; wall-clock gap counts; N_eff of the volume. Left out by design: the
  tradeable-second twins, the LULD halt detector, the 09:45 tape, the EWP speed cells.
- **LegBlock** (`Leg.fs`, 79 columns, one per side): EXT = the side's entry extreme (a new 20m low for a long, high for
  a short), OPP = the opposite one, FLOW = the leg's first EXT event, WT / CT = with / counter ticks. The K ladder
  `k` (reset by a new 20m OPP) and `k_600 … k_30` (reset by a new N-bar OPP; `k_300` = FlushFader's
  lows_since_first_low_300 on a long, SpikeFader's highs_since_first_high_300 on a short); bars / seconds since FLOW;
  trade_idx; the breach counters both ways; the 1m / 2m / 3m OPP extreme (d1m = vwap / opp_60 − 1); the channels;
  the leg-anchored OLS (ssf = ols_slope_since_flow × 6e5, rflow = ols_r_since_flow), eff, eff9 and z; the arming drop
  and its eff metadata; the leg sums (dlv = vwap / (dv_leg / vol_leg) − 1); the tick runs (crf = chg_since_run_first_ext,
  cslu = chg_since_ct). FlushFader's per-bar order: steps 1–4 in Push, the entry snapshot, the resets in Finish.
- **The seal** (`pitfader_seal.fsx`, the 40 sessions): signals identical; **227 features compared, 0 mismatches** —
  longs against FlushFader's fields, shorts against SpikeFader's mirror (a per-side name map in the harness). The 9
  unchecked columns are the delta / 120–180 volume sums (brute-forced, PIT-3). Control: the 300-bar rung broken to reset
  on the 600-bar breach → exactly k_300 and bars_since_first_300 flagged (1,396), restored → 0. Two bugs the seal caught
  on the way: an F# list `[ x; for … -> … ]` silently DROPS x (warning FS0020; the session breach columns went missing —
  the engine now fails if a trip's feature count differs from the names), and SpikeFader's default 540-bar exit
  (set to PitFader's 300 in the harness).
- YM and MHG are left out of the runs (`--exclude-roots`, default YM,MHG; user: "illiquid small contracts").
- **The full run:** mc = 0, 27 roots, 5,719,949 trips, 236 feature columns, 830 s, peak 3.2 GB
  (data/futures/pitfader/trips_mc0, 4.0 GB).
- **The user's equivalence ("Without any features like currently, lows300 would be equivalent to trip depth") HOLDS:**
  `k_300 == open_at_sig` on EVERY trip outside the first episode of its session-side (5,407,356 of 5,719,949 overall).
  The 312,593 exceptions all sit in the session's FIRST episode, where k_300 is larger (never smaller): it also counts
  the leg's new 20m extremes printed before the channel was warm, which are leg events but can't be trips.

## PIT-5 — the greedy gate search on VOLATILITY-NORMALISED features: pooled, then by market group (user 2026-11-01)

The user: "Are there some cheap features like the ones the current FlushFader system has that actually improve net
when added?" — the §S49 greedy, on "vol normalized thresholds for each instrument". `scripts/futures/pitfader_greedy.py`:
the mc = 0 sampler GREEDILY REPLAYED into the mc = 1 book (one trip at a time per root, session, side; a gate is an
entry condition, so the replay of the gated sampler is the gated engine). Every price distance / slope ÷ the root's own
volat_20m at the signal, side-signed so "stretched into the extreme" is negative on both sides; counts, ratios and the
delta imbalance side·delta_N / vol_N as recorded; volatility CAUSALLY as vrel = volat_20m / the root's median over its
previous 20 sessions, and vt = volat_20m in ticks. 31 features × fit-period quantiles 5–95 % × (≥, ≤) = 682 candidate
gates; each step adds the gate that raises the FIT book's net most (Σ bp at equal notional, −1 tick a round trip). FIT
2015–2022, HOLDOUT 2023–2026. ⚠ Memory: the first (pandas) version peaked at 13.6 GB — rewritten to read lean (6.3 GB
pooled, ≤ 3.1 GB per group), and the rewrite reproduces the first run step for step and year for year (control; a
session-key bug — pyarrow dates in seconds, not ns — was caught by that control and fixed).

**Verdict: the POOLED search finds gates that carry out of sample, weakly; the per-group searches overfit.**
- Pooled: the bare mc = 1 book loses (fit PF 0.855, holdout 0.888 net of 1 tick). Step 1 = **k300 ≥ 52** (the depth
  ladder: the 53rd+ fresh extreme since the last 5m bounce) turns it positive (fit 1.11 / holdout 1.02); then **vrel ≥
  0.91** (1.16 / 1.04), **cslu ≥ −0.30** (1.26 / 1.06), and **dimb30 ≤ −0.084 — the VOLUME DELTA**: the last 30 bars sold
  INTO the long / bought into the short (1.315 / **1.097**, the holdout's best step). Steps 5–10 raise only the fit.
- By group the first step is again an EXTREME cell (k600 ≥ 100, k300 ≥ 56, rng20v ≥ p95, dlv ≤ p5, d1m ≤ p5) — trade only
  the extreme flush — but with 682 candidates on a few thousand mc = 1 trips the later steps fit noise: equity 1.227 fit
  vs ~1.00 holdout at every step; energy 1.90 vs 1.00; metals 2.79 vs 0.66; ags 2.31 vs 0.95; VX 3.25 fit vs **0.41**
  holdout (its fit years 2020–22 were the volatile ones). Only FX (holdout 1.11 on 481 trips) and rates (1.11 on 106)
  hold above 1, on too few trips to mean much.
- The equity-index group's BARE book is already ~break-even net of 1 tick (fit 0.983 / holdout 1.002 on 45k trips) —
  the cheapest market to trade relative to its moves; no gate improved its holdout.
⇒ The consistent signal across pooled and group runs is the one the PIT-3 tables showed: depth × volatility, plus the
order-flow agreement (capitulation). The per-instrument configurations need a stronger validation than a forward
greedy over 682 gates (fewer, coarser candidates; a stop rule; a second holdout) — and the 1-tick cost is the size of
the whole gross edge per trade, so the exits (a longer target = a bigger move per trip against the same cost) and the
execution (maker, not taker) are the other levers.

## PIT-6 — the longer target: the exit sweep (user 2026-11-01: "Ok, let's try a longer target")

The engine now records AUX EXITS (`--aux-exit-bars`, default 540, 600, 900, 1200, 1800, 2400, 3600): per trip, the exit at
the first new N-bar opposite extreme at or after its fill bar (next-bar fill) or the session's last bar (eod_N). The
signals and features do not depend on the exit, so one run carries every target
(data/futures/pitfader/trips_mc0_aux, 1,167 s, 3.1 GB peak). Controls: the primary (300) columns == the PIT-4 run
(5,719,949 = 5,719,949, identical); no aux exit before the 300-bar one (0); the seal still 0 mismatches. RAW = next-bar
vwap (the user: "Are we not testing raw signals here?" — yes; the −1 tick is a stress line). `scripts/futures/pitfader_exits.py`.

**Verdict: a longer target HURTS the broad book and HELPS the selective core a little, up to ~900 bars.**
- Bare sampler: PF raw 1.128 (300) → 1.090 (540) → 1.049 (1200) → 1.007 (3600); the mean move per trip does NOT grow
  (+1.36 ticks at 300, +1.43 at 600, +0.29 at 3600) while the eod share climbs to 45 %. The bare mc = 1 book is
  likewise best at 300 (1.051 → 0.984), and 300 is the best exit for 25 of 27 roots. The reversion on the bare signal
  is SHORT-LIVED: holding longer only adds noise.
- The deep, volatile cells (D10, k = 50 / 100) keep their PF and DOUBLE their move per trip with the target (k = 100:
  +34.7 ticks at 300 → +74.2 at 3600) — so against a fixed cost a longer hold pays only there.
- The PIT-7 selective core (k300 ≥ K + the per-root absolute floor, re-chosen on fit per exit), HOLDOUT 2023–26:
  K = 100, mc = 1: 300 → 1.402 raw / 1.288 −1t (1,225 episodes) · 540 → 1.444 / 1.359 (744) · 600 → 1.436 / 1.356 (740)
  · **900 → 1.469 / 1.399 (641)** · 1200 → 1.279 / 1.224 · 1800+ → 1.16–1.27. K = 100, mc = 0: 300 → 1.264 / 1.186 · 540 / 600
  → 1.33–1.34 / 1.28 · 900 → 1.315 / 1.269 · 1200+ → 1.05–1.09. K = 75 at 540 / 600: mc = 0 1.369 / 1.29–1.30 (≈ 1,900 episodes).
  ⇒ for the selective core a 540–900-bar target (the ~9–15m opposite extreme, SpikeFader's region) adds ~+0.07–0.11 to
  the stressed PF; beyond 1,200 bars it gives it back. Fewer episodes at longer exits: a slot stays occupied longer.
- ⚠ The "mean ticks" column mixes roots with very different tick sizes (NQ ticks are 0.25 points), so it can be
  negative beside a PF > 1 (bp, equal notional); the PFs are the comparable numbers.

## PIT-7 — more selective: the depth ladder pushed up, and an ABSOLUTE volatility floor per instrument (user 2026-11-01)

The user, on the mc = 0 holdout: "This is telling us that we need to be more selective in our trading. If needed, we
should push k300 higher ... we need to find a better way of calibrating volatility for each instrument. I am not sure
that vrel in particular is doing a good job. We should find an absolute value on the fit set and see if it works well
on the holdout as well." `scripts/futures/pitfader_select.py` (the 300-bar exit; RAW = next-bar vwap, −1 tick = stress;
fit 2015–22, holdout 2023–26; episodes = the trips of one root-session-side that exit together, the independent unit).
The absolute floor: per root, volat_20m ≥ a FIXED bp value, chosen among the root's fit-period quantiles (0–95 %) as
the one maximising the fit book's Σ (raw − 1 tick) with ≥ 30 fit episodes left; applied unchanged to the holdout (a root
with too few fit episodes stays unfloored, so the compared books hold the same roots — the first version dropped them,
a confound that made the k300 ≥ 100 floor look worse).

**Verdict:**
- **The depth ladder pays up to k300 ≈ 75–100, then breaks.** Holdout mc = 0: K 0 → 1.122 raw · 52 → 1.208 · **75 →
  1.249 (1.152 −1t, 3,884 episodes)** · 100 → 1.224 (1.148, 1,443 ep) · 150 → **0.673** (209 ep) · 200 → 0.436. mc = 1:
  best at **K = 100: 1.329 raw / 1.222 −1t** on 1,443 episodes. Above 100 the fit soars (2.7 at 150, 4.8 at 200) on a
  few hundred episodes and the holdout collapses: too few flushes that deep to calibrate on.
- **The absolute per-root floor beats vrel in all four comparisons, by a little.** k300 ≥ 52: holdout mc = 0 base 1.208
  → vrel 1.256 → **absolute 1.279** (−1t 1.097 → 1.144 → 1.170); mc = 1 1.142 → 1.161 → 1.170. k300 ≥ 100: mc = 0 1.224 →
  1.241 → **1.264**; mc = 1 1.329 → 1.358 → **1.402 (−1t 1.288)**.
- The chosen floors (k300 ≥ 52): ES 1.54 bp (fit p20), NQ 1.62 (p20), MES 1.89 (p30), MGC 3.42 (p60), CL 5.20 (p50), NG
  8.56 (p20), RB 6.66 (p40), VX 16.2 (p10), 6E / 6N / 6S / HG / MCL / ZB at p0 (the depth gate already selects their
  volatile tape). Holdout gains: MES 1.31 → 1.47, MGC 1.33 → 1.92, NQ 1.18 → 1.31, ES 1.19 → 1.32, RB 1.06 → 1.17; loss:
  6A 1.56 → 1.18.
- ⚠ **The ENERGIES fail the holdout at every setting:** CL 0.88 / MCL 0.87 unfloored at k300 ≥ 52 (0.36 / 0.45 at ≥ 100),
  after 1.5 / 1.9 fit. And **2023 is the weak year** of every book (0.95 at ≥ 52, 0.55 at ≥ 100).

## PIT-8 — the volume delta on its own (user 2026-11-01: "I am really surprised that the volume delta doesn't help — have we tested various negative thresholds in the last 30s to 5m?")

Not until now: the delta had only been one of 31 greedy candidates (PIT-5). `scripts/futures/pitfader_delta.py`:
dimb_N = side × delta_N_bar / vol_N_bar over the last N present bars (NEGATIVE = the tape aggressing WITH the flush:
selling into a long, buying into a short), N = 30 … 1200, fixed thresholds ≤ −0.6 … 0 and, for contrast, ≥ 0 / ≥ +0.2;
on (B) k300 ≥ 52 with the 300-bar exit and (C) the PIT-7 core (k300 ≥ 75 + the per-root absolute floor) with the 600-bar
exit; mc = 0 and mc = 1; fit 2015–22 / holdout 2023–26; raw (−1 tick stress).

**Verdict: the delta matters — in the OPPOSITE direction to the capitulation idea, and per market group.**
- At a deep flush the tape is already one-sided: on base B the median dimb_30 is −0.19 (p90 +0.01), dimb_300 −0.09. So
  "the flow agrees with the flush" is the NORMAL state; the informative tail is the ~12 % where it does NOT (dimb ≥ 0:
  a new deep extreme printed on balanced or opposite aggression — the price walking down through passive selling /
  absorbed buying).
- Strong with-the-flush flow FAILS the holdout: base B mc = 0, dimb_30 ≤ −0.6 → fit 1.56 / holdout 0.94; dimb_120 ≤ −0.4 →
  1.65 / 0.85; dimb_300 ≤ −0.3 → 1.18 / 0.82 (base alone 1.383 / 1.208). On core C the same: dimb_180 ≤ −0.3 → 1.21 / 0.67.
- The AGAINST side (dimb ≥ 0) holds or improves the holdout: base B dimb_60 ≥ 0 → **1.362** (vs 1.208), dimb_120 ≥ 0 →
  1.355, dimb_300 ≥ 0 → 1.327; core C dimb_180 ≥ 0 → **1.720 mc = 0** (1.626 −1t, 785 episodes) vs 1.369, mc = 1 1.389
  (vs 1.174); dimb_300 ≥ 0 → 1.541. Its fit is LOWER than the base's, which is why a fit-selected greedy picked the
  other side.
- By group (base B, mc = 0, fit / holdout), dimb_60 ≤ −0.3 vs ≥ 0: **equity indexes with 1.32 / 0.95 · against 1.57 /
  1.45**; FX with 1.80 / 1.12 · against 1.81 / 1.52; rates + ags + VX against 1.68 / 3.33; energy ~flat (1.11 / 0.95 vs
  1.17 / 1.08); metals mixed. dimb_180 ≤ −0.2 vs ≥ 0: equity 1.23 / 1.22 vs **1.54 / 1.59** — the against side wins in
  BOTH periods for the equity indexes. Year by year the against cell is noisier (2017 0.72, 2025 2.43): it is ~12 % of
  the trips.
- The greedy's mc = 1 choice dimb_30 ≤ −0.084 (PIT-5) picks which trip takes the slot; on the mc = 0 sampler it does not
  carry.
⇒ A per-group delta gate — dimb_60 / 180 ≥ 0 for the equity indexes (and FX) — is the robust form; to be confirmed
with the episode count and a per-root look before it goes in a configuration.

#### B: k300 ≥ 52, exit 300

## PIT-9 — bonds split from commodities; the grid per market group (user 2026-11-01: "I'd like to split bond futures from
commodities. Besides equity indices, bonds are what I am most interested in. How does this system perform on them?")

The user on PIT-8: "for once that the bit of market wisdom was true, that a divergence in the volume delta does have
signal." `scripts/futures/pitfader_group.py --group {bonds,sofr,equity,commodities,…}`: depth k300 ≥ K × exit 300 / 600 /
900 × the absolute floor × the delta divergence (dimb_60 / dimb_180 ≥ 0), mc = 0 and mc = 1, fit 2015–22 / holdout
2023–26, raw (−1 tick stress), episodes. Bonds = ZB, ZN, ZT; SR3 (SOFR, a short-rate future) on its own; commodities =
CL, MCL, NG, RB, HG, MGC, ZC, ZS, HE.

**BONDS — the strongest raw edge per trip, the thinnest sample, and a post-2022 regime.**
- The bare bond book has the highest raw PF of any group (fit 1.31 / holdout 1.39, mc = 0) but loses after a tick
  (0.88 / 0.86): 1 tick = 1.40 bp (median), large against the bonds' small moves.
- Deep flushes are RARE in bonds: k300 ≥ 25 = 1,087 fit + 404 holdout episodes, ≥ 52 = 162 + 55, ≥ 75 = 34 + 7. The
  bond depth gate sits near 25 (vs 75–100 for the equity indexes).
- k300 ≥ 25: mc = 0 holdout 2.73 raw / 1.81 −1t (404 ep) · exit 600 → 3.56 / 2.64 · exit 900 → 2.95 / 2.33; mc = 1 1.55
  / 0.96 → 1.73 / 1.21 (600) → 1.66 / 1.25 (900). k300 ≥ 52, exit 900: mc = 1 holdout 3.19 / 2.66 on 55 episodes.
- All three roots carry it (k300 ≥ 25, holdout): ZB 4.39 / 2.68 (140 ep), ZN 1.91 / 1.31 (204), ZT 2.79 / 2.10 (60).
- The delta divergence: k300 ≥ 25 + dimb_60 ≥ 0, exit 600 → mc = 0 holdout 4.29 / 3.12 (177 ep), mc = 1 2.87 / 2.01 — but
  its fit is weak (mc = 1 1.06 / 0.83). The absolute floor does little (most bond floors land at p0).
- ⚠ The holdout BEATS the fit, and by year k300 ≥ 25 runs 1.07–1.83 in 2015–18, then 3.90 (2022), 2.41, 4.14, 2.51:
  the bond edge is the post-2022 rate-volatility regime. Small samples (tens to hundreds of episodes a year).
- SR3: 204 holdout episodes bare; too thin to read.

**EQUITY INDEXES — the smooth, robust group; the delta divergence is their strongest gate.** The depth ladder is
monotone (exit 300, mc = 0 holdout: K 0 → 1.10 · 52 → 1.22 · 75 → 1.29 · 100 → 1.44 / 1.37, 820 ep). k300 ≥ 75 + the
absolute floor + dimb_180 ≥ 0: exit 300 → mc = 0 holdout **2.33 / 2.21 (526 ep)** (vs 1.41 without the delta gate), mc = 1
1.41 / 1.32; exit 600 → mc = 0 **3.93 / 3.88 (216 ep)**, mc = 1 1.67 / 1.63. NQ k300 ≥ 52 + dimb_180 ≥ 0: 1.61 → 1.89 / 1.84.

**COMMODITIES — weaker, and the delta divergence does NOT carry:** k300 ≥ 75 + floor + dimb_180 ≥ 0, exit 300 → holdout 0.65;
CL / MCL fail at depth (k300 ≥ 52: CL 1.35 → 0.88, MCL 1.90 → 0.87); MGC, NG, RB, HG, ZS hold 1.06–1.93 at k300 ≥ 52.

**FX (6A 6B 6C 6E 6J 6N 6S, M6A M6E E7) — the most CONSISTENT group: fit ≈ holdout, positive after a tick at depth, the
300-bar exit.** (Added 2026-11-01, user: "What about FX?")
- Bare: raw 1.20 fit / 1.21 holdout (1.01 / 1.01 −1t; an FX tick is 0.5–0.9 bp). k300 ≥ 25: 1.38 / **1.39** (1.18 −1t,
  3,888 holdout ep) — the fit and the holdout agree. k300 ≥ 52: 1.72 / 1.48 / 1.28 −1t (1,017 ep); mc = 1 1.49 → 1.37 /
  1.17. Deeper (≥ 75, ≥ 100) saturates at ~1.43–1.47 on 330 / 91 episodes.
- The absolute floor helps at mc = 1 (k300 ≥ 52 + floor: holdout 1.48 / 1.29, 719 ep). The delta divergence adds a little
  at the 300-bar exit: k300 ≥ 52 + floor + dimb_180 ≥ 0 → mc = 0 1.71 / 1.49 (372 ep); k300 ≥ 75 + floor + dimb_180 ≥ 0 →
  2.22 / 1.95 (132 ep), mc = 1 1.79 / 1.56.
- 300 bars is the FX exit: 600 / 900 lower the PF at every depth (k300 ≥ 52 mc = 0 holdout 1.48 → 1.24 → 1.14).
- By year (k300 ≥ 25, mc = 0): every year ≥ 1.00 (2021 1.00, 2025 1.16; 2015 2.29, 2026 2.16). k300 ≥ 52: 2021 0.98 and
  2025 1.02 the weak years.
- Per root, k300 ≥ 52 holdout: 6N 3.00, 6J 2.73, 6B 1.91, 6E 1.70, 6C 1.67, 6A 1.56 — and **6S fails** (2.61 fit → 0.81);
  M6A and E7 too thin.

## PIT-10 — is the mc = 0 vs mc = 1 gap an entry-price gap, and would a better (limit) entry close it? (user 2026-11-01:
"I wonder if the huge difference between mc = 0 and mc = 1 for bonds is due to how thick the order book is? Maybe getting
1 or 2 ticks better entry would close the gap between mc = 0 and mc = 1?")

`scripts/futures/pitfader_limit.py` (+ `_deep`: 3–12 ticks). In an mc = 0 episode every later trip is a fresh 20m
extreme, so it enters better than the first. The limit test rests a bid k ticks better than the SIGNAL bar's vwap until
the trip's exit; the path's extreme = the best signal vwap of the later trips of the same root-session-side before that
exit (each fresh extreme is a recorded signal); TOUCH = the 1s vwap reaches the limit, THROUGH = ≥ 1 tick past it (the
thick-book rule: touching does not fill the back of the queue); a miss = no trade.

**Verdict: the gap is the flush CONTINUING, not the book's thickness, and 1–2 ticks do not close it.**
- The later trips of an episode enter far better than its first: bonds +7–9 ticks (median +5–6), FX +14–17 (median +10),
  equity indexes +46–72 (median +23–30). mc = 0 averages in down the whole leg; mc = 1 takes only the first trip
  (bonds k300 ≥ 25, holdout: first trip 1.55 vs the episode 2.73).
- A 1–2-tick better limit, bonds k300 ≥ 25 exit 300, holdout: market 1.55 → −1t touch 1.73 (82 % filled) · −2t 1.79 (69 %)
  — far from mc = 0's 2.73; with the THROUGH rule 1.12 / 1.36; and in the FIT years every limit is no better or worse than
  the market (1.27 → 1.19–1.25 touch, 0.86–0.89 through). Equity and FX: every 1–3-tick limit LOWERS the PF.
- The misses are the instant winners (adverse selection): the trips a −2t bond limit misses have a market PF of 47–88.
- Deeper limits raise the PF of what fills — bonds k300 ≥ 25 exit 300 holdout −8t 3.40 (27 % filled), −12t 5.98 (16 %)
  — by skipping the quick bounces (the misses' PF 11–4): a different, deeper entry, not a better fill of the same one;
  the fit years rise much less (1.48 / 1.79).
⇒ To capture mc = 0's edge with a bounded book, ADD as the flush deepens (a capped averaging-down book, mc = 2–5, or adds
spaced in ticks / volatility units) rather than wait for a better first fill. The engine supports `--max-concurrent k`.

## PIT-11 — the z-score, efficiency and coil features on the FINANCIAL groups (user 2026-11-01: "It really does seem like
there is a lot of truth in Gary Smith's book that traders who make money are those who trade financial assets like
stocks, bonds and currencies instead of commodities. I'd like to test the zscore, eff and coil features on them. Are
there any easy wins to be had using those features?")

`scripts/futures/pitfader_features.py`: each financial group on its PIT-9 core (equity k300 ≥ 75 + abs floor, exit 300;
bonds k300 ≥ 25, exit 600; FX 6A/6B/6C/6E/6J/6N k300 ≥ 52 + abs floor, exit 300 — 6S and the thin micros out); each
feature split into its FIT quintiles (fit → holdout raw PF, holdout episodes, mc = 0), plus the one-sided cut with the
best FIT PF that keeps ≥ 40 % of the fit episodes, and its holdout at mc = 0 and mc = 1. Directional features are
side-signed (negative = into the extreme on both sides).

**Verdict: three easy wins, consistent across groups — a stretched leg z, a persistent trend into the extreme
(eff_ewma), and the LOW end of the coil — mostly at mc = 0.**
- **The leg z (z_since_flow / z_since_opp: the price vs the leg's own volume-weighted distribution) — equity and bonds.**
  Equity z_since_flow quintiles fall in BOTH periods (fit 2.35 → 1.27, holdout 1.56 → 1.29); the fit-chosen cut ≤ −2.09
  → holdout 1.52 (895 ep), z_since_opp ≤ −2.06 → 1.69 / 1.61 (790 ep), mc = 1 1.30 (core 1.41 / mc = 1 1.19). Bonds:
  monotone in the holdout for all three z's (z_since_flow Q1 10.05 → Q5 1.81; fit 2.42 → 1.14); z_since_flow ≤ −2.45 →
  holdout 10.05 / 7.34 (110 ep), **mc = 1 3.52 / 2.44** (core 1.73); z20 ≤ −2.42 → mc = 1 3.08. The 20m z20 does NOT
  carry for equity (holdout Q1 1.23: inverted-U) nor clearly for FX.
- **eff_ewma (the EWMA trend persistence of the slot returns; low = a persistent move into the extreme) — equity and FX.**
  Equity eff_ewma_20m: fit 2.55 → 1.54 and holdout 1.94 → 1.10 across Q1 → Q5; the cut ≤ −0.344 → holdout 1.68 / 1.60
  (670 ep); eff_ewma_10m ≤ −0.42 → 1.60 (1,052 ep), mc = 1 1.27. FX eff_ewma_20m: fit 2.84 → 1.36, holdout 2.45 → 1.95;
  ≤ −0.383 → holdout 2.45 / 2.11 (280 ep), mc = 1 1.89 (core 2.01 / 1.69). Not in bonds.
- **The coil's LOW end (consol_3m / 5m: the last minutes trended rather than coiled) — all three.** Equity
  consol_3m_lag1m ≤ 0.218 → holdout 1.65 / 1.57 (1,301 ep); bonds consol_3m Q1 fit 2.28 → holdout 10.01, cut ≤ 0.138 →
  holdout 5.58, mc = 1 2.35; FX consol_3m holdout monotone (Q1 3.35 → Q5 1.49), ≤ 0.205 → 2.33, mc = 1 1.80. The same
  inversion FlushFader found on stocks (§S49 inversion #8: on the MR side the low end of the coil is the good end).
- Not wins: eff_20m / eff_10m (non-monotone; FX's |eff_20m| ≥ 0.45 jumps in the holdout only, with no fit gain), the
  eff_rng pair, the 10m / 20m coil (equity Q5 holdout 0.92), eff_since_flow / opp (noisy quintiles).
- ⚠ The gains are mostly at mc = 0 (+0.2–0.3 PF); at mc = 1 most cuts move the equity book 1.19 → 1.20–1.30 (bonds and FX
  gain more). Quintile reads looked at the holdout; only the "best cut" column is chosen on fit alone, and about half of
  those improve the holdout (z_since_opp, eff_ewma, consol_3m_lag1m, eff_9ema_20m for equity) while the rest degrade it.

#### equity (ES, MES, NQ) · k300 ≥ 75 + abs floor · exit 300

The core alone: mc = 0 fit 1.88 → holdout **1.41** / 1.34 (1,548 ep); mc = 1 fit 1.49 → holdout **1.19** / 1.11

## PIT-12 — the combined test: a fit-only greedy stack per financial group (user 2026-11-01: "Let's try the combined test group.")

`scripts/futures/pitfader_combo.py`: on each group's PIT-9 core, a greedy stack over the PIT-11 wins (z_since_flow,
z_since_opp, z20 ≤; eff_ewma_20m / 10m ≤; consol_3m, consol_5m, consol_3m_lag1m ≤) and the PIT-8/9 delta divergence (dimb_60
/ dimb_180 ≥), thresholds = the core's FIT quintile edges, each step keeping ≥ 40 % of the core's fit episodes; the holdout
(2023–26) is never used to choose. Shown at mc = 0, mc = 1 and a CAPPED mc = 3 (≤ 3 trips open per root-session-side).
Three objectives, in the order run:
- `net` (Σ(raw − 1 tick) on fit): adds NOTHING to equity or FX — on a core already profitable per trip every gate removes
  positive-expectancy trips; the wrong yardstick for "a better trade" (kept in combo_net.md).
- `pf` (the fit PF): equity picks z20 ≤ −2.13 first (fit 2.53) — the feature PIT-11 showed does NOT carry — and the
  holdout FALLS (1.41 → 1.22). Bonds: z_since_flow ≤ −2.45 (holdout mc = 0 10.05 / 7.34, mc = 1 3.52, mc = 3 4.56 / 3.21, 110 ep).
- ⭐ **`pfmin` = the WORSE of the two fit halves' PF (2015–18, 2019–22): a gate must work in both halves of the fit.**

**Verdict (pfmin): every group gets a stack, chosen on fit alone, that improves its holdout.**

| group | the stack (on the core) | holdout mc 0 raw / −1t (ep) | holdout mc 1 | holdout mc 3 | weakest year (mc 0) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## PIT-13 — commodities searched properly (user 2026-11-01: "Let's switch gears a little and we'll focus on commodities. Can we really not find any edge in them?")

The earlier verdict came from pooling all nine commodity roots through the financial recipes. Now: the PIT-9 grid per
sub-group and root (`pitfader_group.py --group energy|metals|ags`), an energy diagnostic (`pitfader_energy_diag.py`), and
the fit-only stack (`pitfader_combo.py --objective pfmin --both-dirs --groups metals,ags,energy,ngrb`) where the CORE too
is chosen on fit (exit 300 / 600 / 900 × k300 ≥ 25 / 52 / 75 × floor on / off, by the worse fit half, ≥ 150 fit episodes),
every feature is allowed in BOTH directions, and hour-of-day and the scheduled-report hour are candidates. Refactor control:
the equity stack comes out identical.

**Verdict: there IS edge in commodities — metals and natural gas — and the "no edge" was CL / MCL.**
- **Metals (HG, MGC) behave like the financials.** k300 ≥ 52, exit 300, mc = 0: fit 1.41 → holdout 1.34 (1,171 ep); ≥ 75 →
  1.50; ≥ 100 → 2.03 (156 ep). The fit-chosen core (k300 ≥ 52 + abs floor, exit 300): holdout mc = 0 1.48 / 1.38 (477 ep),
  mc = 1 1.23 / 1.14. The feature stack raises the fit but not the holdout: the core alone is the metals configuration.
  MGC the steadier root (1.31 → 1.33 at ≥ 52).
- **Natural gas: a strong, stable stack — with the leg z in the OPPOSITE direction to the financials.** NG + RB, the
  fit-chosen core k300 ≥ 75 + abs floor, exit 900: holdout mc = 0 1.43, mc = 1 1.55 / 1.44 (179 ep); + **z_since_flow ≥ −1.73**
  (the deep leg NOT extremely stretched against its own distribution) → fit 4.20 → **holdout 4.93 / 4.41 (96 ep), mc = 1
  3.50 / 3.14, mc = 3 3.48 / 3.13**; every year 2015–2026 ≥ 2.03; it is NG (3.24 → 4.92, 94 ep). In the financials a
  stretched leg z was the good end; in NG the extreme stretch is the supply shock that keeps going.
- **CL / MCL: unstable, not dead.** By year CL runs 1.60, 1.43, 0.80, 0.99, 1.19, 1.76, 0.79, 1.80 (2015–22), then 0.55,
  0.80, 2.88, 1.39; its sides FLIPPED (long 1.80 → 0.69, short 0.98 → 1.14). ⭐ But the **EIA report hour (Wed 10:30–11:30)
  is the best time to fade it, in both periods**: CL fit 2.15 (120 ep) / holdout 2.96 (43) vs the rest 1.32 / 0.85; MCL
  2.76 / 4.18 vs 1.87 / 0.84; NG's Thursday storage hour 3.42 / 1.75 vs 1.36 / 1.36. The overreaction to the report
  reverts — a candidate event system of its own (small: ~15 CL episodes a year). The fit-only stack did not pick it
  (too few episodes to pass the ≥ 40 % keep rule).
- **Ags (ZC, ZS, HE): edge at depth but too thin to configure** — k300 ≥ 52 1.47 → 1.57 (323 ep), ≥ 75 1.66 → 2.27 (110);
  ZS 1.40 → 1.93; the fit-chosen core has 48 holdout episodes and the stack degrades it.
- Energy pooled (fit-chosen k300 ≥ 75 + floor, exit 900): holdout mc = 1 1.32 / 1.24 (508 ep) — positive, but mc = 0 1.09 and
  the stack worse: CL / MCL drag it.

## PIT-14 — the delta imbalance on every group's core, shown directly (user 2026-11-01: "Does the delta imbalance feature help here? I haven't seen you use it either here or in the combined test group.")

It WAS a candidate in both stacks (PIT-12 / PIT-13: dimb_60, dimb_180 ≥, at 0 and the fit quintiles) — picked for equity
(step 2, dimb_180 ≥ −0.068: the holdout 1.48 → 2.13) and for ags (worse holdout); the greedy found bigger FIT gains
elsewhere for the others. `scripts/futures/pitfader_dimb_groups.py`: on each group's core, dimb_N (N = 30 … 300) by fit
quintile, and the gates dimb_N ≥ 0 (the divergence) vs dimb_N ≤ the fit median (with the flush), mc = 0 and mc = 1.

**Verdict: the divergence helps the equity indexes, bonds and natural gas; FX mildly; in METALS and AGS the sign is
reversed (selling with the flush is the good end — capitulation); and in equity, bonds and NG most of the gain is in the
holdout years, with the fit flat — which is why a fit-only search mostly passed it over.**
- **Equity (k300 ≥ 75 + floor, exit 300):** every window, holdout mc = 0: divergence 1.96–2.59 vs with-the-flush 1.13–1.23
  (core 1.41); mc = 1 1.38–1.71 vs 1.08–1.11 (core 1.19); holdout quintiles monotone (dimb_60 Q1 1.24 → Q5 2.04). The fit is
  flat (1.77–2.10 vs 1.77–1.97).
- **Bonds (k300 ≥ 25, exit 600):** the short windows — dimb_30 ≥ 0 → holdout 5.22 / 3.93, **mc = 1 3.36 / 2.43** (core 3.56 /
  1.73); dimb_30 holdout quintiles 2.12 → 5.18; the fit flat or slightly the other way (1.40 vs 1.57).
- **NG + RB (k300 ≥ 75 + floor, exit 900):** dimb_30 ≥ 0 → holdout 2.26, mc = 1 2.12 (core 1.43 / 1.55); dimb_120 / 180 ≥ 0 →
  2.62 / 2.58; fit ~flat. Energy with CL: dimb_30 ≥ 0 → mc = 1 1.73 vs 1.32.
- **FX:** mild and in both periods — dimb_60 ≥ 0 → mc = 0 2.10 → 2.13, mc = 1 1.83 → 1.89 (core 2.00 → 2.01 / 1.61 → 1.69); Q1
  (most with the flush) the weakest holdout quintile in four of five windows.
- **Metals: REVERSED, in both periods.** Divergence (120–300 bars) fit 0.79–0.91 / holdout 1.21–1.34; with the flush fit 1.66–1.76
  / holdout 1.44–1.55 (core 1.55 / 1.48). Gold and copper revert after real one-sided selling. Ags: the same reversal in the
  holdout (with the flush 9–15, 31–43 episodes: too thin), fit flat.
- **Data check** (the holdout-only pattern could be a change in Sierra's aggressor flags): the median dimb_30 and the share of
  divergent signals are stable in every group across 2015–2026 (equity −0.16 … −0.28, share ≥ 0 5–10 %; no 2023 break). Not a
  data artifact — the effect itself is stronger in 2023–26.

## PIT-15 — what historical CME order-book data would cost for these episodes (user 2026-11-02)

Malcolm asked whether to pay ~\$50 a month for Rithmic's CME market-by-order feed so we could test order-book imbalance
features. Rithmic's history service replays only ticks, tick bars, time bars and minute volume profiles: its protocol has
live depth-by-order messages and a snapshot, but no depth replay. A Rithmic subscription is therefore a LIVE feed that
would have to be recorded for months before a test. Databento's GLBX.MDP3 has the history:

- **MBP-10** (the top 10 levels after every book event, self-contained) and **MBP-1** (top of book with sizes) from 2010-06,
  so the whole 2015–26 sample is covered.
- **MBO** (every order event) from 2017-05-21 only; the book is rebuilt from Databento's 00:00 UTC weekday snapshot, so an
  MBO window must be bought from midnight.

`scripts/futures/pitfader_obi_cost.py` priced it with Databento's free `metadata.get_cost` (nothing bought). The episodes are
each group's core (as PIT-14: k300 + the per-root floor), one window per mc = 0 episode, [the first signal − 20 min, the last
exit], merged per contract-day. 300 windows were priced exactly (≤ 5 per group-year) and scaled by the stratum's window
count. One ES regular session for scale: MBO \$1.47 (0.87 GB), MBP-10 \$2.16 (4.6 GB), MBP-1 \$1.38.

| group | episodes | windows | MBP-10 \$ | MBP-1 \$ | MBO \$ (2017-05-22 on, from midnight) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| equity (ES, MES, NQ) | 3,971 | 3,861 | 673 | 370 | 2,709 |
| bonds (ZB, ZN, ZT) | 1,438 | 1,411 | 72 | 41 | 213 |
| FX (6A 6B 6C 6E 6J 6N) | 2,065 | 2,052 | 51 | 31 | 250 |
| metals (HG, MGC) | 1,113 | 1,103 | 27 | 14 | 247 |
| NG | 380 | 380 | 11 | 4 | 38 |
| **all** | **8,967** | **8,807** | **834** | **460** | **3,457** |

- The windows average 44 minutes; equity is 80 % of the MBP-10 cost (ES and NQ books are the busiest, 2022 the busiest year:
  \$200 of the \$834).
- ⚠ NG's 2025–26 strata failed to price (9 windows): newer NG contracts take a two-digit-year raw symbol (`NGN25`) and
  the script sent `NGN5`. NG's total is low by roughly its 2025–26 share (a few dollars).
- **Without equity, the financial and commodity groups' whole 2015–26 history is about \$160 of MBP-10.** Databento's
  \$125 free credit nearly covers that.
